# Duplicates & data variants merged

[← Test case index](README.md)

Each section lists, per canonical test case, every source test case that was merged into it, the merge type and the reason. A merged row is either a true duplicate or the same check with different data, which automation should run as **one data-driven (parameterized) test** with one row per variant.

**940 source rows merged into 170 canonical cases** (of 599 canonical cases in total).

## Merge types

| Type | Meaning | Rows |
|---|---|---|
| Exact duplicate | Same sheet, same intent, steps and expected result. Adds nothing. | 12 |
| Cross-sheet duplicate | The same check repeated in another sheet. | 11 |
| Data variant | Same steps and expected rule; only the input data differs (currency, category, date, field, bucket, role …). | 294 |
| Matrix combination | One cell of a combination matrix (e.g. transaction type × date × currency), checked by the same rule. | 607 |
| Assertion split | Same screen or flow; the source split the check points into separate rows. One test asserts them all. | 5 |
| Header row | Matrix / section header row with no test of its own. | 11 |

**Verdict** (merge review, 11 Oct 2026): ✅ OK 832 · ⚠️ Check 107 · ✂️ Split 1. ✅ the merge is sound. ⚠️ the merge stands, but the variant has an extra or different expected result that the parameterized test must assert. ✂️ the row tests different behaviour and should become its own case.

## Review result (11 Oct 2026)

Every merged row was re-read against its canonical case (title, preconditions, data, steps, expected result).

- **The merges hold up.** 939 of 940 stay merged. Only one row tests different behaviour and should be split (see the table below). No canonical mixes rows whose outcome is *accepted* with rows whose outcome is *rejected*; the concurrency cases are split correctly by outcome.
- **107 merges need care when automating (⚠️).** The variant stays in the data-driven test, but it adds or changes an assertion. The main patterns:
  - **Other currency / overflow to wallet or deposit.** Also assert the FX conversion and rounding, which currency the wallet is credited in, and that the overflow stays off the SOA balance.
  - **Same-time payment + payment pairs in the SOA matrix** (OB-SOA-011 … 016). If both payments hit the same open invoice, the second is rejected (see 12 Payment Concurrency). Use separate invoices, or assert the rejection.
  - **UO × UO and EM × EM pairs in [OB-SOA-017](features/balances/19-soa-ob-cb.md#-ob-soa-017--soa-obcb-after-one-transaction-type-followed-by-another--type-pair-matrix).** A second UO or EM for the same apartment and period may be blocked (relates to D5). Confirm before automating; split them out if they are blocked.
  - **Assigning an unknown RV on the account statement** ([OB-ACS-001](features/balances/22-account-statement-ob-cb.md#-ob-acs-001--account-statement-obcb-continuity-after-cash-received-rv-current-date--by-rv-type), [OB-ACS-002](features/balances/22-account-statement-ob-cb.md#-ob-acs-002--account-statement-obcb-continuity-after-back-dated-cash-received-rv--by-rv-type)). This reclassifies cash already in the bank, so the statement must show **no second inflow**. The generic expected text would let double counting pass.
  - **Over-balance concurrency** ([OB-WAL-012](features/balances/20-wallet-ob-cb.md#-ob-wal-012--wallet-2-users-debit-fullover-balance-concurrently--second-is-refused), [OB-ACS-010](features/balances/22-account-statement-ob-cb.md#-ob-acs-010--account-2-users-debit-fullover-balance-concurrently--second-is-refused)). Rows differ in when the balance is checked (draft save vs posting) and in the message. Parameterize the type, stage and message.
- **Every OB/CB matrix shares one expected text** ("closing balance of the previous period = opening balance of the next"). The parameterized test must compute the expected balance change per transaction type (charge adds, payment subtracts; Cash In vs Cash Out). Without that, the "balance calculation" check proves nothing.
- **Two SOA canonicals are very wide:** [OB-SOA-014](features/balances/19-soa-ob-cb.md#-ob-soa-014--soa-obcb-with-2-different-type-transactions-at-same-time--current--current-date) (59 rows) and [OB-SOA-017](features/balances/19-soa-ob-cb.md#-ob-soa-017--soa-obcb-after-one-transaction-type-followed-by-another--type-pair-matrix) (130 rows). Keep them as one rule, but consider sub-groups (charge + charge, charge + payment, payment + payment) so a failure is easier to trace.
